# <span class="deva-large">र</span> <span class="iast-large">ra</span>

<div class="dict-page-header">
  <span class="badge-count">537 entrées</span>
  <a href="/" class="back-link">← Index du dictionnaire</a>
</div>

---

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry--ra">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">॰र</span> <i>-ra</i> forme des a. possessifs: qui donne, qui fait, qui utilise, qui possède.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry--ri">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">॰रि</span> <i>-ri</i> forme des a.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry--ru">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">॰रु</span> <i>-ru</i> forme des a.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-_rc_1">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    √ <span class="deva" lang="sa">ऋच्</span> <i>ṛc_1</i> v. [<span class="skt-grammar">1</span>] pr. <i>(arcati)</i> fut. <i>(</i><i>arciṣyati</i><i>)</i> pft. <i>(ānarca)</i> aor. [<span class="skt-grammar">5</span>] <i>(ārcīt)</i> pp. <i>(</i><i>arcita</i><i>)</i> pfp. <i>(</i><i>arcya</i>, <i>arcanīya</i><i>)</i> abs. <i>(</i><i>arcitvā</i>, <i>-arcya</i><i>)</i> véd. briller <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> louer, vénérer, honorer — ca. <i>(arcayati)</i> opt. <i>(arcayet)</i> faire briller; honorer — dés. <i>(arciciṣati)</i> vouloir honorer.<br> <i>nādevo</i> <i>devam</i> <i>arcayet</i> Nul autre que Dieu ne doit adorer Dieu.<br> <i>nārudrastu</i> <i>spṛśedrudraṃ</i> <i>nārudro</i> <i>rudram</i> <i>arcayet</i> [<a href="#entry-Ulifgapuraa_na" class="skt-link">Liṅgapurāṇa</a>] Nul autre que <a href="#entry-Urudra" class="skt-link">Rudra</a> ne doit ondoyer <a href="#entry-Urudra" class="skt-link">Rudra</a>, nul autre que <a href="#entry-Urudra" class="skt-link">Rudra</a> ne doit adorer <a href="#entry-Urudra" class="skt-link">Rudra</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-_rc_2">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">ऋच्</span> <i>ṛc_2</i> [agt. <a href="#entry-_rc_1" class="skt-link">ṛc_1</a>] <a href="#entry-_rc_2" class="skt-link">f.</a> vers sacré, strophe ou stance du <a href="#entry-U_rgveda" class="skt-link">Ṛgveda</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> pl. <i>ṛcas</i> les stances (du <a href="#entry-U_rgveda" class="skt-link">Ṛgveda</a>).
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-ra_mh">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    √ <span class="deva" lang="sa">रंह्</span> <i>raṃh</i> v. [<span class="skt-grammar">1</span>] pr. <i>(raṃhati)</i> pp. <i>(</i><i>raṃhita</i><i>)</i> se hâter, se dépêcher <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> couler, s'écouler.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-ra_n">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    √ <span class="deva" lang="sa">रण्</span> <i>raṇ</i> [relié à <a href="#entry-ram" class="skt-link">ram</a>] v. [<span class="skt-grammar">1</span>] pr. <i>(raṇati)</i> pp. <i>(</i><i>raṇita</i><i>)</i> trouver du plaisir à <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> faire du bruit; résonner.<br> <i>raṇ</i> <i>raṇ</i> [onomatopée] ind. imite le cri not. des grues.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-ra_na">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">रण</span> <i>raṇa</i> [act. <a href="#entry-ra_n" class="skt-link">raṇ</a>] <a href="#entry-ra_na" class="skt-link">m.</a> joie, délice, plaisir — <a href="#entry-ra_na" class="skt-link">n.</a> conflit, combat, bataille, lutte, guerre <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> phil. l'action.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-ra_naarjita">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>raṇārjita</i> [<a href="#entry-arjita" class="skt-link">arjita</a>] a. <a href="#entry-ra_naarjita" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-ra_naarjita" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-ra_naarjitaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>raṇārjitā</i> gagné à la guerre.<br> <i>nātaḥ</i> <i>parataro</i> <i>dharmo</i> <i>nṛpāṇāṃ</i> <i>yad</i> <i>raṇārjitam</i> <i>viprebhyo</i> <i>dīyate</i> <i>dravyam</i> [YjSm.] Il n'y a pas pour un roi de devoir supérieur à celui de donner aux brâhmanes la richesse acquise à la guerre.<br> <a href="#entry-ra_na" class="skt-link">raṇa</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-ra_nya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">रण्य</span> <i>raṇya</i> [pfp. [<span class="skt-grammar">1</span>] <a href="#entry-ra_n" class="skt-link">raṇ</a>] a. <a href="#entry-ra_nya" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-ra_nya" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-ra_nyaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>raṇyā</i> délectable, plaisant <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> prêt au combat — <a href="#entry-ra_nya" class="skt-link">n.</a> joie, plaisir <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> bataille.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_dhaa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राढा</span> <i>rāḍhā</i> var. <i>rārā</i> <a href="#entry-raa_dhaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> beauté, splendeur <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> géo. la région du Bengale.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_naa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राणा</span> <i>rāṇā</i> [pkt. <a href="#entry-raajan" class="skt-link">rājan</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_naa" class="skt-link">m.</a> roi — <a href="#entry-raa_nii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāṇī</i> titre Rāṇī moyen-indien de reine.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_naayana">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राणायन</span> <i>rāṇāyana</i> [vr. <a href="#entry-ra_na" class="skt-link">raṇa</a>-<a href="#entry-aayana" class="skt-link">āyana</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_naayana" class="skt-link">m.</a> patr. de Rāṇāyana «descendant de Raṇa», fondateur d'une école de chant védique.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_naayaniiya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṇāyanīya</i> [<a href="#entry--iiya" class="skt-link">-īya</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raa_naayaniiya" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raa_naayaniiya" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raa_naayaniiyaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāṇāyanīyā</i> de dit de l'école de chant <a href="#entry-Usaamaveda" class="skt-link">Sāmaveda</a> de <a href="#entry-Uraa_naayana" class="skt-link">Rāṇāyana</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raa_naayana" class="skt-link">rāṇāyana</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_tra">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राष्ट्र</span> <i>rāṣṭra</i> [instr. <a href="#entry-raaj_2" class="skt-link">rāj_2</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_tra" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raa_s_tra" class="skt-link">n.</a> royaume, empire; peuple, sujets.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_trabh_rt">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭrabhṛt</i> [<a href="#entry-bh_rt" class="skt-link">bhṛt</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_trabh_rt" class="skt-link">m.</a> soc. véd. épisode rituel du «soutien du royaume» pendant le sacrifice de l'<a href="#entry-agnicayana" class="skt-link">agnicayana</a>; le prêtre <a href="#entry-adhvaryu" class="skt-link">adhvaryu</a> y prononce le <a href="#entry-yajus" class="skt-link">yajus</a> consacrant les 4 classes.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_trabhaa_saa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭrabhāṣā</i> [<a href="#entry-bhaa_saa" class="skt-link">bhāṣā</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_trabhaa_saa" class="skt-link">f.</a> langue du royaume <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> mod. désignation partisane de l'<a href="#entry-Uhindii" class="skt-link">Hindī</a> comme langue de l'Union Indienne.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_trakuu_ta">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭrakūṭa</i> [<a href="#entry-kuu_ta" class="skt-link">kūṭa</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_trakuu_ta" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. de la dynastie Rāṣṭrakūṭa du nord du <a href="#entry-Ukar_naa_taka" class="skt-link">Karṇāṭaka</a> (9-10⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècles); ils patronnèrent la construction des caves d'Ellora; les Rāṣṭrakūṭās cultivaient la culture sanskrite, not. à la cour du roi <a href="#entry-Uk_r_s_na_3" class="skt-link">Kṛṣṇa_3</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_trapa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭrapa</i> [<a href="#entry-pa_2" class="skt-link">pa_2</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_trapa" class="skt-link">m.</a> roi [«protecteur du royaume»].
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_trapaala">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭrapāla</i> [<a href="#entry-paala" class="skt-link">pāla</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_trapaala" class="skt-link">m.</a> roi [«protecteur du royaume»] <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> bd. [<a href="#entry-Umahaayaana" class="skt-link">Mahāyāna</a>] np. du vénérable [<a href="#entry-aayu_smat" class="skt-link">āyuṣmat</a>] Rāṣṭrapāla.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_trapaalaparip_rcchaa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭrapālaparipṛcchā</i> [<a href="#entry-parip_rcchaa" class="skt-link">paripṛcchā</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_trapaalaparip_rcchaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. np. de l'ouvrage [<a href="#entry-suutra" class="skt-link">sūtra</a>] bd. Rāṣṭrapālaparipṛcchā «Questions de <a href="#entry-Uraa_s_trapaala" class="skt-link">Rāṣṭrapāla</a>»; le moine <a href="#entry-Uraa_s_trapaala" class="skt-link">Rāṣṭrapāla</a> y interroge le <a href="#entry-Ubuddha" class="skt-link">Buddha</a> sur les devoirs [<a href="#entry-dharma" class="skt-link">dharma</a>] d'un accompli [<a href="#entry-bodhisattva" class="skt-link">bodhisattva</a>]; ce texte est antérieur au 6⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle.<br> <a href="#entry-raa_s_trapaala" class="skt-link">rāṣṭrapāla</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_trapati">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭrapati</i> [<a href="#entry-pati" class="skt-link">pati</a>] <a href="#entry-raa_s_trapati" class="skt-link">m.</a> souverain <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> mod. gouvernement.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_s_triya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāṣṭriya</i> [<a href="#entry--iya" class="skt-link">-iya</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raa_s_triya" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raa_s_triya" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raa_s_triyaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāṣṭriyā</i> royal <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> mod. national — <a href="#entry-raa_s_triya" class="skt-link">m.</a> var. <i>rāṣṭrīya</i> f. gouverneur de province.<br> <i>rāṣṭriyasaṃskṛtasthāna</i> n. mod. Centre National du Sanskrit.<br> <a href="#entry-raa_s_tra" class="skt-link">rāṣṭra</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_1">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    √ <span class="deva" lang="sa">रा</span> <i>rā_1</i> v. [<span class="skt-grammar">2</span>] pr. <i>(rāti)</i> pp. <i>(</i><i>rāta</i><i>)</i> donner, accorder, conférer.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raa_2">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">रा</span> <i>rā_2</i> [agt. <a href="#entry-raa_1" class="skt-link">rā_1</a>] ifc. <a href="#entry-raa_2" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raa_2" class="skt-link">f.</a> qui donne, qui octroie.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaddha">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राद्ध</span> <i>rāddha</i> [pp. <a href="#entry-raadh" class="skt-link">rādh</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raaddha" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raaddha" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raaddhaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāddhā</i> accompli, réalisé, préparé; prêt; échu à ⟨acc.⟩ <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> fortuné, couronné de succès <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> initié — <a href="#entry-raaddha" class="skt-link">n.</a> (impers.) il a été accompli par ⟨i.⟩.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaddhi">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राद्धि</span> <i>rāddhi</i> [act. <a href="#entry-raadh" class="skt-link">rādh</a>] <a href="#entry-raaddhi" class="skt-link">f.</a> accomplissement; succès, fortune.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raadh">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    √ <span class="deva" lang="sa">राध्</span> <i>rādh</i> v. [<span class="skt-grammar">5</span>] pr. <i>(rādhnoti)</i> v. [<span class="skt-grammar">4</span>] pr. <i>(rādhyati)</i> pr. md. <i>(rādhyate)</i> aor. [<span class="skt-grammar">3</span>] <i>(arīradhat)</i> pp. <i>(</i><i>rāddha</i><i>)</i> pf. <i>(</i><i>apa</i>, <i>ā</i>, <i>vi</i><i>)</i> atteindre, achever, accomplir, réussir — ca. <i>(rādhayati)</i> accomplir, réaliser — dés. <i>(rirādhayiṣati)</i> désirer accomplir; souhaiter réussir.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raadhaa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राधा</span> <i>rādhā</i> [act. <a href="#entry-raadh" class="skt-link">rādh</a>] <a href="#entry-raadhaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> prospérité, succès <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> myth. [GG.] np. de Rādhā «Réussite», bergère [<a href="#entry-gopii" class="skt-link">gopī</a>] favorite du jeune <a href="#entry-Ugopaala" class="skt-link">Gopāla</a>-<a href="#entry-Uk_r_s_na" class="skt-link">Kṛṣṇa</a> à <a href="#entry-Uv_rndaavana" class="skt-link">Vṛndāvana</a>; elle était l'épouse du vacher <a href="#entry-Uayanagho_sa" class="skt-link">Ayanaghoṣa</a>; <a href="#entry-Uv_r_sabhaanu" class="skt-link">Vṛṣabhānu</a> était son père; elle fut déifiée comme <a href="#entry-Uzrii" class="skt-link">Śrī</a> <a href="#entry-Uraadhaa" class="skt-link">Rādhā</a>, symbole de l'Amour divin, par le mouvement dévotionnel [<a href="#entry-bhakti" class="skt-link">bhakti</a>] de <a href="#entry-Ucaitanya" class="skt-link">Caitanya</a>; cf. <a href="#entry-Uraadhikaa" class="skt-link">Rādhikā</a>, <a href="#entry-Uv_rndaa" class="skt-link">Vṛndā</a>, <a href="#entry-Uhlaadinii" class="skt-link">Hlādinī</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> myth. [<a href="#entry-Umahaabhaarata" class="skt-link">Mah.</a>] np. de Rādhā_1, nourrice de <a href="#entry-Ukar_na" class="skt-link">Karṇa</a>, épouse d'<a href="#entry-Uadhiratha" class="skt-link">Adhiratha</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> astr. np. de Rādhā_2 «Réussite», 14⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ astérisme lunaire [<a href="#entry-nak_satra" class="skt-link">nakṣatra</a>], aussi appelée <a href="#entry-Uvizaakhe" class="skt-link">Viśākhe</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raadhaaku_n_da">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rādhākuṇḍa</i> [<a href="#entry-ku_n_da" class="skt-link">kuṇḍa</a>] <a href="#entry-raadhaaku_n_da" class="skt-link">n.</a> myth. np. du Rādhākuṇḍa, étang où se baignait <a href="#entry-Uraadhaa" class="skt-link">Rādhā</a> à <a href="#entry-Uv_rndaavana" class="skt-link">Vṛndāvana</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raadhaa" class="skt-link">rādhā</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raadhana">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राधन</span> <i>rādhana</i> [act. <a href="#entry-raadh" class="skt-link">rādh</a>] <a href="#entry-raadhana" class="skt-link">n.</a> fait de se concilier; prière.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raadhas">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राधस्</span> <i>rādhas</i> [<a href="#entry-raadh" class="skt-link">rādh</a>] <a href="#entry-raadhas" class="skt-link">n.</a> réussite, succès; pouvoir.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raadheya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राधेय</span> <i>rādheya</i> [<a href="#entry-raadhaa" class="skt-link">rādhā</a><a href="#entry--iiya" class="skt-link">-īya</a>] <a href="#entry-raadheya" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. [<a href="#entry-Umahaabhaarata" class="skt-link">Mah.</a>] np. de Rādheya, épith. de <a href="#entry-Ukar_na" class="skt-link">Karṇa</a> «élevé par <a href="#entry-Uraadhaa_1" class="skt-link">Rādhā_1</a>».
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raadhikaa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राधिका</span> <i>rādhikā</i> [<a href="#entry-raadhaa" class="skt-link">rādhā</a><a href="#entry--ika" class="skt-link">-ika</a>] <a href="#entry-raadhikaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> myth. np. de Rādhikā, diminutif de <a href="#entry-Uraadhaa" class="skt-link">Rādhā</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaga">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राग</span> <i>rāga</i> [act. <a href="#entry-ra_nj" class="skt-link">rañj</a>] <a href="#entry-raaga" class="skt-link">m.</a> coloration, rougeur; intensité de rouge; couleur, teinture <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> passion, amour envers ⟨loc.⟩; opp. <a href="#entry-viraaga" class="skt-link">virāga</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> mus. mélodie; mode musical (approprié à une ambiance particulière) <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> assaisonnement; condiment — <a href="#entry-raagii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāgī</i> bot. <i>Eleusine coracana</i>, éleusine, mil rouge.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raagadve_sa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāgadveṣa</i> [<a href="#entry-dve_sa" class="skt-link">dveṣa</a>] <a href="#entry-raagadve_sa" class="skt-link">m.</a> du. attachement et aversion.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raagamaalaa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāgamālā</i> [<a href="#entry-maalaa" class="skt-link">mālā</a>] <a href="#entry-raagamaalaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. association des modes musicaux [<a href="#entry-raaga" class="skt-link">rāga</a>] à des heures, des saisons, des émotions, etc.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raagama_njarii">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāgamañjarī</i> [<a href="#entry-ma_njarii" class="skt-link">mañjarī</a>] <a href="#entry-raagama_njarii" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. [DKC.] np. de Rāgamañjarī «Bouquet de mélodies», sœur de <a href="#entry-Ukaamama_njarii" class="skt-link">Kāmamañjarī</a>; malgré les remontrances de sa mère, elle décida de choisir son mari sur ses vertus et non sur sa richesse.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raagavibodha">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāgavibodha</i> [<a href="#entry-vibodha_2" class="skt-link">vibodha_2</a>] <a href="#entry-raagavibodha" class="skt-link">m.</a> lit. np. du Rāgavibodha, manuel de musique versifié, dû à <a href="#entry-Usomanaatha_1" class="skt-link">Somanātha_1</a> (1609); il décrit les sortes de luths [<a href="#entry-vii_naa" class="skt-link">vīṇā</a>] et explique la manière d'en jouer; il traite des modes musicaux [<a href="#entry-raaga" class="skt-link">rāga</a>] qu'il classifie en géniteurs [<a href="#entry-janaka" class="skt-link">janaka</a>] et dérivés [<a href="#entry-janya" class="skt-link">janya</a>]; il donne des mélodies.<br> <a href="#entry-raaga" class="skt-link">rāga</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaghava">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राघव</span> <i>rāghava</i> [vr. <a href="#entry-raghu" class="skt-link">raghu</a>] <a href="#entry-raaghava" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. np. Rāghava «descendant de <a href="#entry-Uraghu" class="skt-link">Raghu</a>»; patr. not. de <a href="#entry-Udazaratha" class="skt-link">Daśaratha</a>, de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> et de ses frères <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. de Rāghava_1 ou Rāghavadeva, <a href="#entry-raajaguru" class="skt-link">rājaguru</a> du roi <a href="#entry-Uhammiira" class="skt-link">Hammīra</a> (13⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle) <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> pl. <i>rāghavās</i> myth. [<a href="#entry-Uraamaaya_na" class="skt-link">Rām.</a>] <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>, ses frères et leur partisans.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaghavayaadaviiya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāghavayādavīya</i> [<a href="#entry-yaadaviiya" class="skt-link">yādavīya</a>] <a href="#entry-raaghavayaadaviiya" class="skt-link">m.</a> lit. np. du Rāghavayādavīya «Histoire de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> et <a href="#entry-Uk_r_s_na" class="skt-link">Kṛṣṇa</a>», poème à deux interprétations (<a href="#entry-anulomaviloma" class="skt-link">anulomaviloma</a>); il en existe plusieurs versions.<br> <a href="#entry-raaghava" class="skt-link">rāghava</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaghaviiya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राघवीय</span> <i>rāghavīya</i> [<a href="#entry-raaghava" class="skt-link">rāghava</a><a href="#entry--iiya" class="skt-link">-īya</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raaghaviiya" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raaghaviiya" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raaghaviiyaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāghavīyā</i> à propos de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raagin">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">रागिन्</span> <i>rāgin</i> [agt. <a href="#entry-ra_nj" class="skt-link">rañj</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raagin" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raagin" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raagi_nii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāgiṇī</i> rouge <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> passionné pour, épris de, aimant ⟨loc.⟩ — ifc. qui charme — <a href="#entry-raagin" class="skt-link">m.</a> amant — <a href="#entry-raagi_nii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāgiṇī</i> modification d'un mode musical, personnifiée comme son amante; on en compte 35 ou 36 <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> myth. np. de Rāgiṇī «Amante», fille aînée de <a href="#entry-Uhimavaan" class="skt-link">Himavān</a> et de <a href="#entry-Umenaa" class="skt-link">Menā</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raahu">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राहु</span> <i>rāhu</i> <a href="#entry-raahu" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. np. de l'influence [<a href="#entry-graha" class="skt-link">graha</a>] Rāhu, dragon <a href="#entry-daitya" class="skt-link">daitya</a> qui dévore le Soleil ou la Lune durant les éclipses; il est fils de <a href="#entry-Uvipracitti" class="skt-link">Vipracitti</a> et <a href="#entry-Usi_mhikaa" class="skt-link">Siṃhikā</a> [<a href="#entry-Usai_mhikeya" class="skt-link">Saiṃhikeya</a>]; il fut décapité par <a href="#entry-Uvi_s_nu" class="skt-link">Viṣṇu</a> alors qu'il buvait l'<a href="#entry-am_rta" class="skt-link">amṛta</a> qu'il avait dérobé lors du <a href="#entry-k_siirodamathana" class="skt-link">kṣīrodamathana</a>, et seule sa tête est immortelle; sa queue est devenue le 9⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ <a href="#entry-graha" class="skt-link">graha</a> <a href="#entry-Uketu" class="skt-link">Ketu</a>; on le représente par une grosse tête avec la bouche ouverte; son corps périssable <a href="#entry-Uketu" class="skt-link">Ketu</a> est représenté en serpent; il est <a href="#entry-Udaanavapati" class="skt-link">Dānavapati</a>, roi des ondins [<a href="#entry-daanavaas" class="skt-link">dānavās</a>]; cf. <a href="#entry-Ujalandhara" class="skt-link">Jalandhara</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raahugama">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāhugama</i> [<a href="#entry-gama" class="skt-link">gama</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raahugama" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raahugama" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raahugamaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāhugamā</i> éclipsé; assombri.<br> <a href="#entry-raahu" class="skt-link">rāhu</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raahuketu">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāhuketu</i> [<a href="#entry-ketu" class="skt-link">ketu</a>] <a href="#entry-raahuketu" class="skt-link">m.</a> du. <i>rāhuketū</i> myth. np. de Rāhuketū, les deux influences [<a href="#entry-graha" class="skt-link">graha</a>] <a href="#entry-Uraahu" class="skt-link">Rāhu</a> et <a href="#entry-Uketu" class="skt-link">Ketu</a> ensemble; cf. <a href="#entry-k_siirodamathana" class="skt-link">kṣīrodamathana</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raahula">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राहुल</span> <i>rāhula</i> <a href="#entry-raahula" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. de Rāhula, fils du prince <a href="#entry-Usiddhaartha" class="skt-link">Siddhārtha</a> <a href="#entry-Ugautama" class="skt-link">Gautama</a> (futur <a href="#entry-Ubuddha" class="skt-link">Buddha</a>) et de <a href="#entry-Uyazodharaa" class="skt-link">Yaśodharā</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaj_1">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    √ <span class="deva" lang="sa">राज्</span> <i>rāj_1</i> v. [<span class="skt-grammar">1</span>] pr. <i>(rājati)</i> pr. md. <i>(rājate)</i> pp. <i>(</i><i>rājita</i><i>)</i> pf. <i>(</i><i>nis</i>, <i>vi</i>, <i>sam</i><i>)</i> briller, resplendir <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> être roi; régner sur ⟨g. acc.⟩, gouverner — ca. <i>(rājayati)</i> régner.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaj_2">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राज्</span> <i>rāj_2</i> [agt. <a href="#entry-raaj_1" class="skt-link">rāj_1</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raaj_2" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raaj_2" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raaj_2" class="skt-link">f.</a> ifc. qui brille — <a href="#entry-raaj_2" class="skt-link">m.</a> roi, chef; <a href="#entry-k_satriya" class="skt-link">kṣatriya</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> lat. <i>rex</i>; all. Reich; ang. rich; fr. roi, riche.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaja">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राज</span> <i>rāja</i> iic. de <a href="#entry-raajan" class="skt-link">rājan</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaanaka">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājānaka</i> <a href="#entry-raajaanaka" class="skt-link">m.</a> roitelet.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaasandii">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājāsandī</i> [<a href="#entry-aasandii" class="skt-link">āsandī</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaasandii" class="skt-link">f.</a> soc. [«trône royal»] tabouret tressé portant les tiges de <a href="#entry-soma" class="skt-link">soma</a> dans les rites véd. solennels.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaavali">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājāvali</i> [<a href="#entry-aavali" class="skt-link">āvali</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaavali" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. np. de l'épopée dynastique du <a href="#entry-Ukazmiira" class="skt-link">Kaśmīra</a> Rājāvali (ou Rājāvalī) de <a href="#entry-Ujonaraaja" class="skt-link">Jonarāja</a> (1412); cf. <a href="#entry-Udvitiiyaraajatarafgi_nii" class="skt-link">Dvitīyarājataraṅgiṇī</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaavalipataakaa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājāvalipatākā</i> [<a href="#entry-pataakaa" class="skt-link">patākā</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaavalipataakaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. np. de l'épopée dynastique du <a href="#entry-Ukazmiira" class="skt-link">Kaśmīra</a> Rājāvalipatākā de <a href="#entry-Upraajya" class="skt-link">Prājya</a> <a href="#entry-Ubha_t_ta" class="skt-link">Bhaṭṭa</a>, suite de la <a href="#entry-Uraajatarafgi_nii" class="skt-link">Rājataraṅgiṇī</a>; elle s'arrête à la conquête du <a href="#entry-Ukazmiira" class="skt-link">Kaśmīra</a> par l'empereur Akbar.<br> <a href="#entry-raajaavali" class="skt-link">rājāvali</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaavarta">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājāvarta</i> [<a href="#entry-aavarta" class="skt-link">āvarta</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaavarta" class="skt-link">m.</a> lapis-lazuli.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajabhoja">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājabhoja</i> [<a href="#entry-bhoja" class="skt-link">bhoja</a>] <a href="#entry-raajabhoja" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. du roi du <a href="#entry-Umaalava" class="skt-link">Mālava</a> Bhoja_1; cf. <a href="#entry-Ubhojadeva" class="skt-link">Bhojadeva</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajada_n_da">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājadaṇḍa</i> [<a href="#entry-da_n_da" class="skt-link">daṇḍa</a>] <a href="#entry-raajada_n_da" class="skt-link">m.</a> soc. sceptre.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajadanta">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājadanta</i> [<a href="#entry-danta" class="skt-link">danta</a>] <a href="#entry-raajadanta" class="skt-link">m.</a> [«dent principale»] dent de devant <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> gram. exemple générique d'un composé à dépendance inversée.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajadharma">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājadharma</i> [<a href="#entry-dharma" class="skt-link">dharma</a>] <a href="#entry-raajadharma" class="skt-link">m.</a> devoir du roi <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> lit. [<a href="#entry-Umahaabhaarata" class="skt-link">Mah.</a>] np. de la section Rājadharma du <a href="#entry-zaantiparvan" class="skt-link">śāntiparvan</a> traitant des devoirs d'un roi <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> mod. politique.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajadhuraa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājadhurā</i> [<a href="#entry-dhuraa" class="skt-link">dhurā</a>] <a href="#entry-raajadhuraa" class="skt-link">f.</a> soc. charge royale.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajadroha">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājadroha</i> [<a href="#entry-droha" class="skt-link">droha</a>] <a href="#entry-raajadroha" class="skt-link">m.</a> opression, tyrannie <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> rebellion, sédition.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajadrohin">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājadrohin</i> [<a href="#entry--in" class="skt-link">-in</a>] <a href="#entry-raajadrohin" class="skt-link">m.</a> rebelle; traître.<br> <a href="#entry-raajadroha" class="skt-link">rājadroha</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajag_rha">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājagṛha</i> [<a href="#entry-g_rha" class="skt-link">gṛha</a>] <a href="#entry-raajag_rha" class="skt-link">n.</a> palais royal <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. de la ville de Rājagṛha «Résidence royale», ancienne capitale du <a href="#entry-Umagadha" class="skt-link">Magadha</a> (avant le 5⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle ant.); on dit qu'elle fut fondée par <a href="#entry-Ujaraasandha" class="skt-link">Jarāsandha</a> comme <a href="#entry-Ugirivraja" class="skt-link">Girivraja</a>; c'était un centre important de bouddhisme, proche de <a href="#entry-Unaalandaa" class="skt-link">Nālandā</a>; mod. Rājgīr au <a href="#entry-Ubihaara" class="skt-link">Bihāra</a> — a. <a href="#entry-raajag_rha" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajag_rha" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajag_rhii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājagṛhī</i> habitant de <a href="#entry-Uraajag_rha" class="skt-link">Rājagṛha</a> — <a href="#entry-raajag_rhii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājagṛhī</i> np. de Rājagṛhī, ancien nom de la ville <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> pali <i>rājagaha</i>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaghaataka">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājaghātaka</i> [<a href="#entry-ghaataka" class="skt-link">ghātaka</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaghaataka" class="skt-link">m.</a> régicide.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajagopaala">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājagopāla</i> [<a href="#entry-gopaala" class="skt-link">gopāla</a>] <a href="#entry-raajagopaala" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. np. de Rājagopāla «Roi bouvier», aspect de <a href="#entry-Uk_r_s_na" class="skt-link">Kṛṣṇa</a>-<a href="#entry-Uvi_s_nu" class="skt-link">Viṣṇu</a> adoré not. à Mannargudi au Tamil Nadu.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaguru">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājaguru</i> [<a href="#entry-guru" class="skt-link">guru</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaguru" class="skt-link">m.</a> soc. guide spirituel du roi.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaha_msa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājahaṃsa</i> [<a href="#entry-ha_msa" class="skt-link">haṃsa</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaha_msa" class="skt-link">m.</a> natu. zoo. <i>Cignus cignus</i>, cygne; zoo. <i>Phoenicopterus roseus</i>, flamant rose <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> lit. [DKC.] np. du roi du <a href="#entry-Umagadha" class="skt-link">Magadha</a> Rājahaṃsa «Cygne parmi les rois»; il fut détrôné par son rival <a href="#entry-Umaanasaara" class="skt-link">Mānasāra</a>; son fils <a href="#entry-Uraajavaahana" class="skt-link">Rājavāhana</a> (de son épouse <a href="#entry-Uvasumatii" class="skt-link">Vasumatī</a>) le vengea.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaka">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजक</span> <i>rājaka</i> [agt. <a href="#entry-raaj_1" class="skt-link">rāj_1</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaka" class="skt-link">m.</a> roi.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajakarma">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājakarma</i> [<a href="#entry-karma" class="skt-link">karma</a>] <a href="#entry-raajakarma" class="skt-link">n.</a> soc. destinée royale <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> pl. <i>rājakarmāṇi</i> soc. rituels de réinstallation d'un roi exilé (élection du <a href="#entry-purohita" class="skt-link">purohita</a>, préparations guerrières, consécration du roi, jeu de dé établissant sa supériorité).
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajakoza">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājakośa</i> [<a href="#entry-koza" class="skt-link">kośa</a>] <a href="#entry-raajakoza" class="skt-link">m.</a> dictionnaire royal.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajakozanigha_n_tu">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājakośanighaṇṭu</i> [<a href="#entry-nigha_n_tu" class="skt-link">nighaṇṭu</a>] <a href="#entry-raajakozanigha_n_tu" class="skt-link">m.</a> lit. np. du Rājakośanighaṇṭu «Glossaire royal»; cf. <a href="#entry-raajavyavahaarakoza" class="skt-link">rājavyavahārakośa</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raajakoza" class="skt-link">rājakośa</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajakula">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājakula</i> [<a href="#entry-kula" class="skt-link">kula</a>] <a href="#entry-raajakula" class="skt-link">n.</a> famille royale <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> palais royal <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> rue principale, voix royale.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajakulamahaamantrin">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājakulamahāmantrin</i> [<a href="#entry-mahaamantrin" class="skt-link">mahāmantrin</a>] <a href="#entry-raajakulamahaamantrin" class="skt-link">m.</a> soc. titre de majordome du palais, ministre royal principal.<br> <a href="#entry-raajakula" class="skt-link">rājakula</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaliilaa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājalīlā</i> [<a href="#entry-liilaa" class="skt-link">līlā</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaliilaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> délassement du roi.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaliilaasana">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājalīlāsana</i> [<a href="#entry-aasana" class="skt-link">āsana</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaliilaasana" class="skt-link">n.</a> posture assise d'aise royale, une jambe repliée horizontalement, l'autre repliée verticalement, les pieds se touchant.<br> <a href="#entry-raajaliilaa" class="skt-link">rājalīlā</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajama_ni">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājamaṇi</i> [<a href="#entry-ma_ni" class="skt-link">maṇi</a>] <a href="#entry-raajama_ni" class="skt-link">m.</a> joyau royal <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> pierre précieuse.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajamaarga">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājamārga</i> [<a href="#entry-maarga" class="skt-link">mārga</a>] <a href="#entry-raajamaarga" class="skt-link">m.</a> voie royale.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajamaarta_n_da">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājamārtaṇḍa</i> [<a href="#entry-maarta_n_da" class="skt-link">mārtaṇḍa</a>] <a href="#entry-raajamaarta_n_da" class="skt-link">m.</a> lit. np. du Rājamārtaṇḍa «Lumière royale (sur le <a href="#entry-yoga" class="skt-link">yoga</a>)», commentaire sur le <a href="#entry-Uyogasuutra" class="skt-link">Yogasūtra</a> de <a href="#entry-Upata_njali_2" class="skt-link">Patañjali_2</a> [<a href="#entry-Upaata_njalav_rtti" class="skt-link">Pātañjalavṛtti</a>] dû au roi <a href="#entry-Ubhojadeva" class="skt-link">Bhojadeva</a>; cf. <a href="#entry-Ubhojav_rtti" class="skt-link">Bhojavṛtti</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajamahita">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājamahita</i> [<a href="#entry-mahita" class="skt-link">mahita</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajamahita" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajamahita" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajamahitaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājamahitā</i> honoré par le roi.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajan">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजन्</span> <i>rājan</i> [<a href="#entry-raajat" class="skt-link">rājat</a>] <a href="#entry-raajan" class="skt-link">m.</a> roi, chef, dieu; <a href="#entry-k_satriya" class="skt-link">kṣatriya</a>, noble <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> soc. np. de Rājā, titre de Roi <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> le roi aux échecs [<a href="#entry-caturafgaa" class="skt-link">caturaṅgā</a>]; syn. <a href="#entry-n_rpa" class="skt-link">nṛpa</a> — <a href="#entry-raaj_nii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājñī</i> reine — <a href="#entry-raaja" class="skt-link">m.</a> <i>rāja</i> ifc. roi des — v. [<span class="skt-grammar">11</span>] pr. <i>(rājīyati)</i> souhaiter un roi <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> se comporter comme un roi.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajana">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजन</span> <i>rājana</i> [<a href="#entry-raajan" class="skt-link">rājan</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajana" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajana" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajanii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājanī</i> appartenant à une famille royale.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajanya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजन्य</span> <i>rājanya</i> [<a href="#entry-raajan" class="skt-link">rājan</a><a href="#entry--ya" class="skt-link">-ya</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajanya" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajanya" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajanyaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājanyā</i> royal — <a href="#entry-raajanya" class="skt-link">m.</a> homme de caste <a href="#entry-k_satriya" class="skt-link">kṣatriya</a>, noble; prince.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajanyabandhu">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājanyabandhu</i> [<a href="#entry-bandhu" class="skt-link">bandhu</a>] <a href="#entry-raajanyabandhu" class="skt-link">m.</a> prince de deuxième rang.<br> <a href="#entry-raajanya" class="skt-link">rājanya</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajapuru_sa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājapuruṣa</i> [<a href="#entry-puru_sa" class="skt-link">puruṣa</a>] <a href="#entry-raajapuru_sa" class="skt-link">m.</a> la personne du roi <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> serviteur du roi <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> gram. [générique] composé qui peut s'interpréter soit comme composé descriptif [<a href="#entry-karmadhaaraya" class="skt-link">karmadhāraya</a>] soit comme composé déterminatif casuel [<a href="#entry-tatpuru_sa" class="skt-link">tatpuruṣa</a>]; dans le premier cas le composant de gauche (ici <a href="#entry-raajan" class="skt-link">rājan</a>) est principal [<a href="#entry-pradhaana" class="skt-link">pradhāna</a>] et celui de droite (ici <a href="#entry-puru_sa" class="skt-link">puruṣa</a>) est auxiliaire [<a href="#entry-upasarjana" class="skt-link">upasarjana</a>]; dans le second cas c'est l'inverse.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaputra">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājaputra</i> [<a href="#entry-putra" class="skt-link">putra</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaputra" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajaputrii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājaputrī</i> [«fils de roi»] membre de la caste <a href="#entry-k_satriya" class="skt-link">kṣatriya</a>; prince, noble <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hi. <i>rājput</i>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaputriiya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājaputrīya</i> [<a href="#entry--iiya" class="skt-link">-īya</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaputriiya" class="skt-link">m.</a> traité médical sur le traitement des éléphants, attribué à <a href="#entry-Ubudha" class="skt-link">Budha</a> (fils de <a href="#entry-Ucandra" class="skt-link">Candra</a>-<a href="#entry-Utaarakaaraaja" class="skt-link">Tārakārāja</a>).
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajar_sabha">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājarṣabha</i> [<a href="#entry-_r_sabha" class="skt-link">ṛṣabha</a>] <a href="#entry-raajar_sabha" class="skt-link">m.</a> meilleur parmi les rois.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajar_si">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājarṣi</i> [<a href="#entry-_r_si" class="skt-link">ṛṣi</a>] <a href="#entry-raajar_si" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. sage [<a href="#entry-_r_si" class="skt-link">ṛṣi</a>] d'origine royale; cf. <a href="#entry-Ujanakaraaja" class="skt-link">Janakarāja</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaraa_nii">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājarāṇī</i> [<a href="#entry-raa_nii" class="skt-link">rāṇī</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaraa_nii" class="skt-link">f.</a> géo. np. du temple de Rājarāṇī de <a href="#entry-Ubhuvanezvarii_1" class="skt-link">Bhuvaneśvarī_1</a> (11⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle), de plan <a href="#entry-pa_ncaratha" class="skt-link">pañcaratha</a>, sanctuaire d'Indreśvara-<a href="#entry-Uziva" class="skt-link">Śiva</a>, en grès rose Rājarāṇīya.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaraaja">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājarāja</i> [<a href="#entry-raaja" class="skt-link">rāja</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaraaja" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. np de Rājarāja «Roi des rois», un titre de <a href="#entry-Ukubera" class="skt-link">Kubera</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. du roi <a href="#entry-Ucola" class="skt-link">Cola</a> Rājarāja_1 (985–1012); sa capitale était Tanjavur (mod. Tamil Nadu); cf. <a href="#entry-b_rhadiizvara" class="skt-link">bṛhadīśvara</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajaraajezvara">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājarājeśvara</i> [<a href="#entry-iizvara" class="skt-link">īśvara</a>] <a href="#entry-raajaraajezvara" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. np. de Rājarājeśvara, épith. de <a href="#entry-Uziva" class="skt-link">Śiva</a> «Empereur» — <a href="#entry-raajaraajezvarii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājarājeśvarī</i> myth. np. de Rājarājeśvarī, aspect terrible [<a href="#entry-dazamahaavidyaa" class="skt-link">daśamahāvidyā</a>] de la Déesse; cf. <a href="#entry-U_so_dazii" class="skt-link">Ṣoḍaśī</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raajaraaja" class="skt-link">rājarāja</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajasa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजस</span> <i>rājasa</i> [vr. <a href="#entry-rajas" class="skt-link">rajas</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajasa" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajasa" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajasaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājasā</i> [«dont le <a href="#entry-gu_na" class="skt-link">guṇa</a> prépondérant est <a href="#entry-rajas" class="skt-link">rajas</a>»] entreprenant, actif, énergique; passionné, animé par la passion.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajasika">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājasika</i> [<a href="#entry--ika" class="skt-link">-ika</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajasika" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajasika" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajasikaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājasikā</i> id.<br> <a href="#entry-raajasa" class="skt-link">rājasa</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajasuuya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājasūya</i> [<a href="#entry-suuya" class="skt-link">sūya</a>] <a href="#entry-raajasuuya" class="skt-link">m.</a> soc. cérémonie solennelle de consécration royale, sacre.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajat">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजत्</span> <i>rājat</i> [ppr. <a href="#entry-raaj_1" class="skt-link">rāj_1</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajat" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajat" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajantii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājantī</i> régnant.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajatarafgi_nii">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājataraṅgiṇī</i> [<a href="#entry-tarafgi_nii" class="skt-link">taraṅgiṇī</a>] <a href="#entry-raajatarafgi_nii" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. np. de la Rājataraṅgiṇī «Rivière des rois», épopée historique en 8000 vers de <a href="#entry-Ukalha_na" class="skt-link">Kalhaṇa</a> contant les exploits des rois <a href="#entry-Ulalitaaditya" class="skt-link">Lalitāditya</a> et <a href="#entry-Ujayaapii_da" class="skt-link">Jayāpīḍa</a>, dans un style de sanskrit personnel plein d'émotions mais historiquement fiable (1148); elle fut complétée par la <a href="#entry-Udvitiiyaraajatarafgi_nii" class="skt-link">Dvitīyarājataraṅgiṇī</a> de <a href="#entry-Ujonaraaja" class="skt-link">Jonarāja</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajatas">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājatas</i> [<a href="#entry--tas" class="skt-link">-tas</a>] adv. (provenant) du roi; par ordre du roi.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajavaahana">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājavāhana</i> [<a href="#entry-vaahana" class="skt-link">vāhana</a>] <a href="#entry-raajavaahana" class="skt-link">m.</a> lit. [DKC.] np. du prince Rājavāhana «Porteur de royauté», fils de <a href="#entry-Uraajaha_msa" class="skt-link">Rājahaṃsa</a> et de <a href="#entry-Uvasumatii" class="skt-link">Vasumatī</a>; il dirige le groupe des 10 princes dont le <a href="#entry-Udazakumaaracarita" class="skt-link">Daśakumāracarita</a> raconte les aventures.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajavyavahaara">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājavyavahāra</i> [<a href="#entry-vyavahaara" class="skt-link">vyavahāra</a>] iic. de patronage royal.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajavyavahaarakoza">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājavyavahārakośa</i> [<a href="#entry-koza" class="skt-link">kośa</a>] <a href="#entry-raajavyavahaarakoza" class="skt-link">m.</a> lit. np. du Rājavyavahārakośa «Dictionnaire de commission royale», dû à <a href="#entry-Uraghunaatha_3" class="skt-link">Raghunātha_3</a> <a href="#entry-Upa_n_dita" class="skt-link">Paṇḍita</a>; il fut commandé par le roi Marathe <a href="#entry-Uchattrapati" class="skt-link">Chattrapati</a> <a href="#entry-Uzivajii" class="skt-link">Śivajī</a> vers 1675; il donne les équivalents sanskrits des vocables usuels empruntés à l'arabe et au persan; aussi appelé <a href="#entry-Uraajakozanigha_n_tu" class="skt-link">Rājakośanighaṇṭu</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raajavyavahaara" class="skt-link">rājavyavahāra</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajayoga">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājayoga</i> [<a href="#entry-yoga" class="skt-link">yoga</a>] <a href="#entry-raajayoga" class="skt-link">m.</a> soc. <a href="#entry-yoga" class="skt-link">yoga</a> intégral, discipline spirituelle; il mène à la libération [<a href="#entry-kaivalyamukti" class="skt-link">kaivalyamukti</a>]; cf. <a href="#entry-a_s_taafgayoga" class="skt-link">aṣṭāṅgayoga</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajazaasana">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājaśāsana</i> [<a href="#entry-zaasana" class="skt-link">śāsana</a>] <a href="#entry-raajazaasana" class="skt-link">n.</a> soc. édit royal; instruction sur le devoir royal.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajazekhara">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājaśekhara</i> [<a href="#entry-zekhara" class="skt-link">śekhara</a>] <a href="#entry-raajazekhara" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. de Rājaśekhara <a href="#entry-Uyaayaavara" class="skt-link">Yāyāvara</a> «Diadême Royal», poète de la fin du 9⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle, il est l'auteur du traité de poétique <a href="#entry-Ukaavyamiimaa_msaa" class="skt-link">Kāvyamīmāṃsā</a>, du drame Karpūramañjarī en prakrit et des pièces <a href="#entry-Ubaalaraamaaya_na" class="skt-link">Bālarāmāyaṇa</a> et Viddhaśālabhañjikā en sanskrit; son <a href="#entry-mahaaprabandha" class="skt-link">mahāprabandha</a> <a href="#entry-Uharavilaasa" class="skt-link">Haravilāsa</a> lui valut le titre de <a href="#entry-Umahaakavi" class="skt-link">Mahākavi</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. de Rājaśekhara_1, roi de Pandalam au <a href="#entry-Ukerala" class="skt-link">Kerala</a>, descendant de la dynastie <a href="#entry-Upaa_n_dya" class="skt-link">Pāṇḍya</a>; myth. il trouva l'enfant <a href="#entry-Uayonija" class="skt-link">Ayonija</a> sur les berges de la <a href="#entry-Upampaa" class="skt-link">Pampā</a> et l'adopta comme son héritier; c'est l'origine d'Ayyappā, objet d'un culte important à Sabarimala au <a href="#entry-Ukerala" class="skt-link">Kerala</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajendra">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājendra</i> [<a href="#entry-indra" class="skt-link">indra</a>] <a href="#entry-raajendra" class="skt-link">m.</a> roi des rois, empereur.<br> <a href="#entry-raaja" class="skt-link">rāja</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raaji">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजि</span> <i>rāji</i> <a href="#entry-raaji" class="skt-link">f.</a> ligne, trait; rangée, série <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> raie dans les cheveux; ligne de poils <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> var. <i>rājī</i> id.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajiiva">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājīva</i> a. <a href="#entry-raajiiva" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajiiva" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajiivaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājīvā</i> rayé, muni de rayures — <a href="#entry-raajiiva" class="skt-link">m.</a> natu. antilope rayée — <a href="#entry-raajiiva" class="skt-link">n.</a> fleur de lotus bleue.<br> <a href="#entry-raaji" class="skt-link">rāji</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajika">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राजिक</span> <i>rājika</i> [<a href="#entry-raaj_2" class="skt-link">rāj_2</a><a href="#entry--ika" class="skt-link">-ika</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajika" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajika" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajikaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājikā</i> royal — <a href="#entry-raajika" class="skt-link">n.</a> grain de moutarde noire; mesure de poids minime (1/3 <a href="#entry-sar_sapa" class="skt-link">sarṣapa</a>) — <a href="#entry-raajikaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājikā</i> natu. bot. <i>Sinapis ramosa</i>, moutarde noire.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajya">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राज्य</span> <i>rājya</i> [pfp. [<span class="skt-grammar">1</span>] <a href="#entry-raaj_1" class="skt-link">rāj_1</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajya" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajya" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajyaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājyā</i> princier, royal — <a href="#entry-raajya" class="skt-link">n.</a> royauté, empire, gouvernement <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> royaume — <a href="#entry-raajya" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. du prince Rājya <a href="#entry-Uvardhana_1" class="skt-link">Vardhana_1</a>, fils aîné du roi <a href="#entry-Uprabhaakara_1" class="skt-link">Prabhākara_1</a> de <a href="#entry-Usthaanezvara" class="skt-link">Sthāneśvara</a>; il succéda à son père à sa mort en 605; il combattit les Huns [<a href="#entry-huu_naas" class="skt-link">hūṇās</a>] dans le Nord et les extermina; et combattit le roi <a href="#entry-Udevagupta" class="skt-link">Devagupta</a> du <a href="#entry-Umaalava" class="skt-link">Mālava</a> qui avait emprisonné sa sœur Rājyaśrī après avoir tué son époux Grahavarmā; il fut tué traîtreusement par <a href="#entry-Uzazaafka" class="skt-link">Śaśāṅka</a> roi du <a href="#entry-Ugau_da" class="skt-link">Gauḍa</a>; son frère cadet <a href="#entry-Uhar_sa" class="skt-link">Harṣa</a> lui succéda en 606 et s'imposa comme empereur.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raajyavardhana">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rājyavardhana</i> [<a href="#entry-vardhana" class="skt-link">vardhana</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raajyavardhana" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raajyavardhana" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raajyavardhanii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rājyavardhanī</i> qui fait prospérer le royaume — <a href="#entry-raajyavardhana" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. du roi Rājyavardhana, frère aîné du roi <a href="#entry-Uhar_sa" class="skt-link">Harṣa</a>; cf. <a href="#entry-Uraajya" class="skt-link">Rājya</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raajya" class="skt-link">rājya</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raak_sasa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राक्षस</span> <i>rākṣasa</i> [vr. <a href="#entry-rak_sas" class="skt-link">rakṣas</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raak_sasa" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raak_sasa" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raak_sasii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rākṣasī</i> myth. relatif au <a href="#entry-rak_sas" class="skt-link">rakṣas</a> — <a href="#entry-raak_sasa" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. un ogre-vampire du <a href="#entry-rak_sas" class="skt-link">rakṣas</a>; les <i>rākṣasās</i> descendent de <a href="#entry-Unir_rti" class="skt-link">Nirṛti</a> [<a href="#entry-nair_rta" class="skt-link">nairṛta</a>]; ces géants errent la nuit dans les lieux déserts, ils ont des formes terrifiantes, des crocs pointus et la langue pendante; ils mangent la chair crue; leur chef est <a href="#entry-Uraava_na" class="skt-link">Rāvaṇa</a>; leur direction préférée est l'Ouest; ils guettent les fautes d'exécution dans les rites des mortels; cf. <a href="#entry-Ukirmiira" class="skt-link">Kirmīra</a>, <a href="#entry-Ukumbhakar_na" class="skt-link">Kumbhakarṇa</a>, <a href="#entry-Ubaka_1" class="skt-link">Baka_1</a>, <a href="#entry-Uhi_dimba" class="skt-link">Hiḍimba</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> astr. np. de Rākṣasa «Mort-vivant», personnifiant l'an 49 d'un cycle de vie [<a href="#entry-sa_mvatsara" class="skt-link">saṃvatsara</a>] <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. du ministre Rākṣasa_1 des <a href="#entry-Unanda" class="skt-link">Nanda</a> au <a href="#entry-Umagadha" class="skt-link">Magadha</a>; il favorisa le coup d'état de <a href="#entry-Ucandragupta" class="skt-link">Candragupta</a> sous l'influence de <a href="#entry-Ucaa_nakya" class="skt-link">Cāṇakya</a>; cf. <a href="#entry-Umudraaraak_sasa" class="skt-link">Mudrārākṣasa</a> — <a href="#entry-raak_sasii" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rākṣasī</i> myth. ogre-vampire femelle; cf. <a href="#entry-Ukarka_tii" class="skt-link">Karkaṭī</a>, <a href="#entry-Ukaikasii" class="skt-link">Kaikasī</a>, <a href="#entry-Ujaraa" class="skt-link">Jarā</a>, <a href="#entry-Utaa_takaa" class="skt-link">Tāṭakā</a>, <a href="#entry-Udiirghajihvii" class="skt-link">Dīrghajihvī</a>, <a href="#entry-Uzuurpa_nakhaa" class="skt-link">Śūrpaṇakhā</a>, <a href="#entry-Uhi_dimbaa" class="skt-link">Hiḍimbā</a>, <a href="#entry-Uholikaa" class="skt-link">Holikā</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raak_sasaantaka">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rākṣasāntaka</i> [<a href="#entry-antaka" class="skt-link">antaka</a>] <a href="#entry-raak_sasaantaka" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. [<a href="#entry-Uraamaaya_na" class="skt-link">Rām.</a>] np. de Rākṣasāntaka, épith. de <a href="#entry-Uhanumaan" class="skt-link">Hanumān</a> «Tueur de vampires».<br> <a href="#entry-raak_sasa" class="skt-link">rākṣasa</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raak_sasagraha">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rākṣasagraha</i> [<a href="#entry-graha" class="skt-link">graha</a>] <a href="#entry-raak_sasagraha" class="skt-link">m.</a> phil. [<a href="#entry-tantra" class="skt-link">tantra</a>] possession par un vampire.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raala">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राल</span> <i>rāla</i> var. <i>rāli</i> <a href="#entry-raala" class="skt-link">m.</a> natu. résine de l'arbre <a href="#entry-zaala" class="skt-link">śāla</a>, utilisée comme laque.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raama">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">राम</span> <i>rāma</i> [agt. <a href="#entry-ram" class="skt-link">ram</a>] a. <a href="#entry-raama" class="skt-link">m.</a> <a href="#entry-raama" class="skt-link">n.</a> <a href="#entry-raamaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāmā</i> agréable, charmant; plaisant, beau — <a href="#entry-raama" class="skt-link">m.</a> plaisir <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> myth. [<a href="#entry-Uraamaaya_na" class="skt-link">Rām.</a>] np. du roi Rāma «le Charmant», 7⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ <a href="#entry-avataara" class="skt-link">avatāra</a> de <a href="#entry-Uvi_s_nu" class="skt-link">Viṣṇu</a> au <a href="#entry-tretaayuga" class="skt-link">tretāyuga</a>; fils de <a href="#entry-Udazaratha" class="skt-link">Daśaratha</a> roi d'<a href="#entry-Uayodhyaa" class="skt-link">Ayodhyā</a> de la lignée solaire [<a href="#entry-suuryava_mza" class="skt-link">sūryavaṃśa</a>] et de la reine <a href="#entry-Ukausalyaa" class="skt-link">Kausalyā</a>, il est né à midi le 9⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ jour du mois <a href="#entry-caitra" class="skt-link">caitra</a> sous le <a href="#entry-nak_satra" class="skt-link">nakṣatra</a> <a href="#entry-punarvasu" class="skt-link">punarvasu</a>; son <a href="#entry-guru" class="skt-link">guru</a> était <a href="#entry-Uvizvaamitra" class="skt-link">Viśvāmitra</a>, avec qui il se rendit à <a href="#entry-Umithilaa" class="skt-link">Mithilā</a>; il épousa <a href="#entry-Usiitaa" class="skt-link">Sītā</a>, fille du roi <a href="#entry-Ujanakaraaja" class="skt-link">Janakarāja</a>, en gagnant un concours d'archerie, où il banda, et cassa, l'arc <a href="#entry-Uajagava" class="skt-link">Ajagava</a> de <a href="#entry-Uziva" class="skt-link">Śiva</a>; <a href="#entry-Uparazuraama" class="skt-link">Paraśurāma</a> lui donna l'arc de <a href="#entry-Uvi_s_nu" class="skt-link">Viṣṇu</a>; <a href="#entry-Usiitaa" class="skt-link">Sītā</a> lui donna pour fils <a href="#entry-Ukuza" class="skt-link">Kuśa</a> et <a href="#entry-Ulava" class="skt-link">Lava</a>; <a href="#entry-Udazaratha" class="skt-link">Daśaratha</a> sur ses vieux jours fit de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> son héritier; mais sa seconde femme <a href="#entry-Ukaikeyii" class="skt-link">Kaikeyī</a>, lui rappelant sa promesse d'exhaucer un vœu, l'obligea à laisser le royaume à son fils <a href="#entry-Ubharata_1" class="skt-link">Bharata_1</a>; <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> s'exila volontairement avec <a href="#entry-Usiitaa" class="skt-link">Sītā</a> et son frère <a href="#entry-Ulak_sma_na" class="skt-link">Lakṣmaṇa</a>, <a href="#entry-Ubharata_1" class="skt-link">Bharata_1</a> assurant la régence; <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> vécut avec eux comme ermite dans la forêt <a href="#entry-Uda_n_daka" class="skt-link">Daṇḍaka</a>, dans un ermitage sur la rive de la <a href="#entry-Ugodaavarii" class="skt-link">Godāvarī</a>, où il tua de nombreux démons; l'ogre [<a href="#entry-raak_sasa" class="skt-link">rākṣasa</a>] <a href="#entry-Uraava_na" class="skt-link">Rāvaṇa</a>, roi de <a href="#entry-Ulafkaa" class="skt-link">Laṅkā</a>, décida de les venger, et déguisé en ascète, enleva <a href="#entry-Usiitaa" class="skt-link">Sītā</a> et la transporta à <a href="#entry-Ulafkaa" class="skt-link">Laṅkā</a> dans son char volant [<a href="#entry-vimaana_1" class="skt-link">vimāna_1</a>]; <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> et <a href="#entry-Ulak_sma_na" class="skt-link">Lakṣmaṇa</a> partirent à sa recherche avec l'aide du roi des singes <a href="#entry-Usugriiva" class="skt-link">Sugrīva</a> et de son général le fidèle <a href="#entry-Uhanumaan" class="skt-link">Hanumān</a>, qui franchit le détroit en volant et retrouva <a href="#entry-Usiitaa" class="skt-link">Sītā</a>; l'armée des singes construisit une jetée de pierres [<a href="#entry-setu" class="skt-link">setu</a>] pour franchir l'océan, et après une grande bataille <a href="#entry-Uraava_na" class="skt-link">Rāvaṇa</a> fut vaincu par <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>; cette victoire est célébrée à la fête de <a href="#entry-dazaaha" class="skt-link">daśāha</a>; <a href="#entry-Usiitaa" class="skt-link">Sītā</a>, répudiée par <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>, se jeta au bûcher, mais <a href="#entry-Uagni" class="skt-link">Agni</a> l'épargna, et elle fut réunie à <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>, qui reprit son trône à <a href="#entry-Uayodhyaa" class="skt-link">Ayodhyā</a> pour un long règne de justice; <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> est l'archétype du Roi Juste de Droit Divin; cf. <a href="#entry-Ukaru_nasaagara" class="skt-link">Karuṇasāgara</a>, <a href="#entry-Ukaakutstha" class="skt-link">Kākutstha</a>, <a href="#entry-Udaazaratha" class="skt-link">Dāśaratha</a>, <a href="#entry-Uraghunandana" class="skt-link">Raghunandana</a>, <a href="#entry-Uraghunaatha" class="skt-link">Raghunātha</a>, <a href="#entry-Uraaghava" class="skt-link">Rāghava</a>, <a href="#entry-Uraamacandra" class="skt-link">Rāmacandra</a>, <a href="#entry-Umaariica" class="skt-link">Mārīca</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> [<i>paraśurāma</i>] myth. np. de <a href="#entry-Uparazuraama" class="skt-link">Paraśurāma</a> «Rāma_1 à la hache»; cf. <a href="#entry-Ujaamadagnya" class="skt-link">Jāmadagnya</a> — <a href="#entry-raamaa" class="skt-link">f.</a> <i>rāmā</i> femme charmante.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raama_na">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <span class="deva" lang="sa">रामण</span> <i>rāmaṇa</i> <a href="#entry-raama_na" class="skt-link">m.</a> mod. np. du mystique Rāmaṇa <a href="#entry-Umahar_si" class="skt-link">Maharṣi</a>, né Veṅkaṭa Rāmaṇa Aiyer près de Madurai (1879–1950); il fonda un <a href="#entry-aazrama" class="skt-link">āśrama</a> à <a href="#entry-Uaru_naacala" class="skt-link">Aruṇācala</a> au Tamil Nadu, où il vécut de l'âge de 16 ans à sa mort.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamaananda">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmānanda</i> [<a href="#entry-aananda" class="skt-link">ānanda</a>] <a href="#entry-raamaananda" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. de Rāmānanda «Joie de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>», brâhmane <a href="#entry-vai_s_nava" class="skt-link">vaiṣṇava</a> (~1380–1450), né à <a href="#entry-Uprayaaga" class="skt-link">Prayāga</a>; il popularisa la doctrine <a href="#entry-bhakti" class="skt-link">bhakti</a>; il tenta de concilier l'Islam et l'Hindouisme, et d'abroger les distinctions de caste; Sant Kabir était son disciple, et continua son œuvre de réforme; le poète <a href="#entry-Utulasiidaasa" class="skt-link">Tulasīdāsa</a> appartint à son mouvement; Guru <a href="#entry-Unaanaka" class="skt-link">Nānaka</a> Shah, fondateur de la religion Sikh, s'en inspira <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. de Rāmānanda_1 <a href="#entry-Usvaamii" class="skt-link">Svāmī</a>, né Ajaya <a href="#entry-Uzarmaa" class="skt-link">Śarmā</a> à <a href="#entry-Uayodhyaa" class="skt-link">Ayodhyā</a> en 1738; considéré comme une incarnation d'<a href="#entry-Uuddhava" class="skt-link">Uddhava</a>, il mena une vie d'ascète; il rencontra en rêve son maître <a href="#entry-Uraamaanujaacaarya" class="skt-link">Rāmānujācārya</a>; il fonda l'ordre religieux [<a href="#entry-sampradaaya" class="skt-link">saṃpradāya</a>] <a href="#entry-Uuddhava_1" class="skt-link">Uddhava_1</a> au <a href="#entry-Ugurjara" class="skt-link">Gurjara</a>; son successeur fut son élève <a href="#entry-Usvaamiinaaraaya_na" class="skt-link">Svāmīnārāyaṇa</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. de Rāmānanda_2 <a href="#entry-Usarasvatii_2" class="skt-link">Sarasvatī_2</a> <a href="#entry-Usvaamii" class="skt-link">Svāmī</a> (1867–1936), auteur de la <a href="#entry-Uma_niprabhaa" class="skt-link">Maṇiprabhā</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamaanandin">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmānandin</i> [<a href="#entry--in" class="skt-link">-in</a>] <a href="#entry-raamaanandin" class="skt-link">m.</a> soc. renonçant [<a href="#entry-tyaagin" class="skt-link">tyāgin</a>] disciple de <a href="#entry-Uraamaanuja" class="skt-link">Rāmānuja</a>; pratiquant le <a href="#entry-ha_thayoga" class="skt-link">haṭhayoga</a>; <a href="#entry-Uraamaananda" class="skt-link">Rāmānanda</a> créa leur <a href="#entry-sampradaaya" class="skt-link">sampradāya</a> Rāmānandī; ils adorent <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>-<a href="#entry-Uvi_s_nu" class="skt-link">Viṣṇu</a>, mais aussi <a href="#entry-Uziva" class="skt-link">Śiva</a>; cf. <a href="#entry-Uraamataapinyupani_sad" class="skt-link">Rāmatāpinyupaniṣad</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raamaananda" class="skt-link">rāmānanda</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamaanuja">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmānuja</i> [<a href="#entry-anuja" class="skt-link">anuja</a>] <a href="#entry-raamaanuja" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. épith. de <a href="#entry-Uk_r_s_na" class="skt-link">Kṛṣṇa</a> comme frère cadet de <a href="#entry-Ubalaraama" class="skt-link">Balarāma</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> phil. np. de Rāmānuja «<a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> junior», philosophe et mystique <a href="#entry-vai_s_nava" class="skt-link">vaiṣṇava</a> (~1050–1137) né Ilaya Perumal au Tamil Nadu; marié jeune, il s'établit à <a href="#entry-Ukaa_nciipuram" class="skt-link">Kāñcīpuram</a>, où il rencontra son premier <a href="#entry-guru" class="skt-link">guru</a>, l'<a href="#entry-advaitavaadin" class="skt-link">advaitavādin</a> <a href="#entry-Uyaadavaprakaaza" class="skt-link">Yādavaprakāśa</a>; il le quitta pour <a href="#entry-Uyamunaacaarya" class="skt-link">Yamunācārya</a>, qui prêchait la doctrine de la non-dualité distinguée [<a href="#entry-vizi_s_taadvaita" class="skt-link">viśiṣṭādvaita</a>] qui raffinait la non-dualité simple [<a href="#entry-advaita" class="skt-link">advaita</a>] de <a href="#entry-Uzafkara_1" class="skt-link">Śaṅkara_1</a>; il renvoya sa femme à sa famille pour devenir <a href="#entry-sannyaasin" class="skt-link">sannyāsin</a>, et il vécut à <a href="#entry-Uzriirafga" class="skt-link">Śrīraṅga</a> où il prêcha la libération par la dévotion [<a href="#entry-bhakti" class="skt-link">bhakti</a>] dans la soumission [<a href="#entry-prapatti" class="skt-link">prapatti</a>]; il devint le Maître Rāmānujācārya, et eut de nombreux disciples, not. <a href="#entry-Unaathamuni" class="skt-link">Nāthamuni</a>; il admettait dans son ordre les hors-castes; il fonda 700 monastères dans le <a href="#entry-Ukar_naa_taka" class="skt-link">Karṇāṭaka</a>, établit le temple de <a href="#entry-Uvefka_ta" class="skt-link">Veṅkaṭa</a> à Tirupati, et celui de Tirunārāyaṇa à Melukoṭe près de mod. Mysore; il écrivit de nombreux ouvrages [<a href="#entry-navaratna" class="skt-link">navaratna</a>] sur le <a href="#entry-vedaanta" class="skt-link">vedānta</a>, not. le <a href="#entry-Uzriibhaa_sya" class="skt-link">Śrībhāṣya</a>, le <a href="#entry-Uvedaantasaara_1" class="skt-link">Vedāntasāra_1</a>, le <a href="#entry-Uvedaantadiipa" class="skt-link">Vedāntadīpa</a> et le <a href="#entry-Uvedaarthasafgraha" class="skt-link">Vedārthasaṃgraha</a>; on lui doit aussi le <a href="#entry-Utantrarahasya" class="skt-link">Tantrarahasya</a> et le <a href="#entry-Unityagrantha" class="skt-link">Nityagrantha</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamaaya_na">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmāyaṇa</i> [<a href="#entry-ayana" class="skt-link">ayana</a>] <a href="#entry-raamaaya_na" class="skt-link">n.</a> lit. le Rāmāyaṇa, ou geste de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>, grand poème épique [<a href="#entry-itihaasa" class="skt-link">itihāsa</a>] attribué à <a href="#entry-Uvaalmiiki" class="skt-link">Vālmīki</a>; sa composition remonte au 3⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle ant.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamaaya_naama_njarii">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmāyaṇāmañjarī</i> [<a href="#entry-ma_njarii" class="skt-link">mañjarī</a>] <a href="#entry-raamaaya_naama_njarii" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. np. du poème Rāmayaṇamañjarī, version du <a href="#entry-Uraamaaya_na" class="skt-link">Rām.</a> due à <a href="#entry-Uk_semendra" class="skt-link">Kṣemendra</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raamaaya_na" class="skt-link">rāmāyaṇa</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamaaya_nacampuu">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmāyaṇacampū</i> [<a href="#entry-campuu" class="skt-link">campū</a>] <a href="#entry-raamaaya_nacampuu" class="skt-link">f.</a> lit. np. du Rāmāyaṇacampū, poème classique de <a href="#entry-Ubhojadeva" class="skt-link">Bhojadeva</a> en style <a href="#entry-campuu" class="skt-link">campū</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamabhadra">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmabhadra</i> [<a href="#entry-bhadra" class="skt-link">bhadra</a>] <a href="#entry-raamabhadra" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. np. de Rāmabhadra, épith. de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> cher à <a href="#entry-Udazaratha" class="skt-link">Daśaratha</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. du logicien Rāmabhadra_1 <a href="#entry-Usaarvabhauma" class="skt-link">Sārvabhauma</a>, fils de <a href="#entry-Ujanakiinaatha" class="skt-link">Janakīnātha</a> <a href="#entry-Ucuu_daama_ni" class="skt-link">Cūḍāmaṇi</a> (16⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle); il est auteur d'un commentaire du <a href="#entry-Upadaarthatattvaniruupa_na" class="skt-link">Padārthatattvanirūpaṇa</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. de nombreux autres personnages.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamacandra">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmacandra</i> [<a href="#entry-candra" class="skt-link">candra</a>] <a href="#entry-raamacandra" class="skt-link">m.</a> myth. np. du roi divin Rāmacandra, fils de <a href="#entry-Udazaratha" class="skt-link">Daśaratha</a>; cf. <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. du roi Rāmacandra_1, de la dynastie des <a href="#entry-Uyaadavaas" class="skt-link">Yādavās</a>; il régna à <a href="#entry-Udevagiri" class="skt-link">Devagiri</a> de 1271 à 1309; il fut défait par Ala-ud-din Khalji; son premier ministre était le lettré <a href="#entry-Uhemaadri_1" class="skt-link">Hemādri_1</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> hist. np. du grammairien Rāmacandra_2 du 15⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle, auteur de la <a href="#entry-Uprakriyaakaumudii" class="skt-link">Prakriyākaumudī</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamacandrapura">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmacandrapura</i> [<a href="#entry-pura" class="skt-link">pura</a>] <a href="#entry-raamacandrapura" class="skt-link">n.</a> géo. np. de Rāmacandrapura, ville d'<a href="#entry-Uandhra" class="skt-link">Andhra</a> au nord du delta de la <a href="#entry-Ugodaavarii" class="skt-link">Godāvarī</a>; on y trouve le temple de <a href="#entry-Usomaraama" class="skt-link">Somarāma</a>; c'est un <a href="#entry-pa_ncaraamak_setra" class="skt-link">pañcarāmakṣetra</a>, on dit que le <a href="#entry-zivalifga" class="skt-link">śivaliṅga</a> <a href="#entry-Usomezvara" class="skt-link">Someśvara</a> y fut installé par <a href="#entry-Ucandra" class="skt-link">Candra</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raamacandra" class="skt-link">rāmacandra</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamacarita">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmacarita</i> [<a href="#entry-carita" class="skt-link">carita</a>] <a href="#entry-raamacarita" class="skt-link">n.</a> lit. np. du Rāmacarita «Geste de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>», en 40 chants, composé par Abhinanda; il se base sur le <a href="#entry-Uraamaaya_na" class="skt-link">Rāmāyaṇa</a>, mais introduit des variantes <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> lit. np. du Rāmacarita_1, poème sanskrit à double sens dû à <a href="#entry-Usandhyaakaranandii" class="skt-link">Sandhyākaranandī</a> il glorifie à la fois le dieu <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> et le roi <a href="#entry-Uraamapaala" class="skt-link">Rāmapāla</a>; aussi appelé <a href="#entry-Uraamapaalacarita" class="skt-link">Rāmapālacarita</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamacaritamaanasa">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmacaritamānasa</i> [<a href="#entry-maanasa" class="skt-link">mānasa</a>] <a href="#entry-raamacaritamaanasa" class="skt-link">n.</a> lit. np. du Rāmacaritamānasa «Recueil sacré de la geste de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>», épopée de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> en <a href="#entry-Uhindii" class="skt-link">Hindī</a> due à <a href="#entry-Utulasiidaasa" class="skt-link">Tulasīdāsa</a>.<br> <a href="#entry-raamacarita" class="skt-link">rāmacarita</a>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamagiri">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmagiri</i> [<a href="#entry-giri" class="skt-link">giri</a>] <a href="#entry-raamagiri" class="skt-link">m.</a> géo. np. de la colline de Rāmagiri, près de Nāgapūra au <a href="#entry-Uvidarbha" class="skt-link">Vidarbha</a> (mod. Ramtek au <a href="#entry-Umahaaraa_s_tra" class="skt-link">Mahārāṣṭra</a>).
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamagupta">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmagupta</i> [<a href="#entry-gupta" class="skt-link">gupta</a>] <a href="#entry-raamagupta" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. de Rāmagupta, fils aîné de l'empereur <a href="#entry-Usamudragupta" class="skt-link">Samudragupta</a>; il régna brièvement (375–~380); selon la pièce <a href="#entry-Udeviicandragupta" class="skt-link">Devīcandragupta</a>, il avait lâchement abandonné son épouse <a href="#entry-Udhruvadevii" class="skt-link">Dhruvadevī</a> capturée par un prince <a href="#entry-zaka" class="skt-link">śaka</a> ennemi; son frère cadet Kumāra <a href="#entry-Ucandragupta" class="skt-link">Candragupta</a>, déguisé en femme, s'introduisit dans le camp ennemi, châtia le rival, délivra <a href="#entry-Udhruvadevii" class="skt-link">Dhruvadevī</a>, puis pour l'épouser assassina <a href="#entry-Uraamagupta" class="skt-link">Rāmagupta</a>, et monta sur le trône sous le nom de <a href="#entry-Ucandragupta_2" class="skt-link">Candragupta_2</a> (vers 380).
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamak_r_s_na">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmakṛṣṇa</i> [<a href="#entry-k_r_s_na" class="skt-link">kṛṣṇa</a>] <a href="#entry-raamak_r_s_na" class="skt-link">m.</a> mod. np. du saint bengali <a href="#entry-zrii" class="skt-link">śrī</a> Rāmakṛṣṇa <a href="#entry-paramaha_msa" class="skt-link">paramahaṃsa</a>, né Thākūr (1836–1886); il prêcha une doctrine syncrétique combinant le <a href="#entry-vedaanta" class="skt-link">vedānta</a> et la <a href="#entry-bhakti" class="skt-link">bhakti</a>; il fonda un <a href="#entry-aazrama" class="skt-link">āśrama</a> à Belūr près de Calcutta; son principal disciple fut <a href="#entry-Uvivekaananda" class="skt-link">Vivekānanda</a>, qui fonda la mission <a href="#entry-Uraamak_r_s_na" class="skt-link">Rāmakṛṣṇa</a> en 1897 <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> [<i>rāmakṛṣṇakāvya</i>] lit. np. du poème Rāmakṛṣṇakāvya, dû à <a href="#entry-Usuuryadaasa" class="skt-link">Sūryadāsa</a>; c'est un <a href="#entry-vilomaak_sarakaavya" class="skt-link">vilomākṣarakāvya</a> de 36 vers; chaque vers a 4 pieds [<a href="#entry-cara_na" class="skt-link">caraṇa</a>], les deux premiers ont pour sujet <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a>, et les deux suivants, obtenus en renversant l'ordre de leurs syllabes, ont pour sujet <a href="#entry-Uk_r_s_na" class="skt-link">Kṛṣṇa</a> <span class="skt-pipe">|</span> du. <i>rāmakṛṣṇau</i> myth. <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> et <a href="#entry-Uk_r_s_na" class="skt-link">Kṛṣṇa</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamak_setra">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmakṣetra</i> [<a href="#entry-k_setra" class="skt-link">kṣetra</a>] <a href="#entry-raamak_setra" class="skt-link">n.</a> géo. l'un des 5 lieux de pélerinage de <a href="#entry-Uraama" class="skt-link">Rāma</a> en pays <a href="#entry-Uandhra" class="skt-link">Andhra</a>: <a href="#entry-Uamaraavatii" class="skt-link">Amarāvatī</a>, <a href="#entry-Udraak_saaraama" class="skt-link">Drākṣārāma</a>, <a href="#entry-Usomaraama" class="skt-link">Somarāma</a>, <a href="#entry-Uk_siiraraama" class="skt-link">Kṣīrarāma</a> et <a href="#entry-Ukumaararaama" class="skt-link">Kumārarāma</a>; myth. l'<a href="#entry-asura" class="skt-link">asura</a> <a href="#entry-Utaaraka" class="skt-link">Tāraka</a> possédait un <a href="#entry-lifga" class="skt-link">liṅga</a> qui lui donnait la toute-puissance; <a href="#entry-Ukumaara" class="skt-link">Kumāra</a> le cassa en 5 morceaux, qui tombèrent sur Terre aux 5 endroits, et furent établis comme <a href="#entry-zivalifga" class="skt-link">śivaliṅga</a> par respectivement <a href="#entry-Uindra" class="skt-link">Indra</a>, <a href="#entry-Usuurya" class="skt-link">Sūrya</a>, <a href="#entry-Ucandra" class="skt-link">Candra</a>, <a href="#entry-Uvi_s_nu" class="skt-link">Viṣṇu</a> et <a href="#entry-Ukumaara" class="skt-link">Kumāra</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamaka_n_tha">
  <div class="skt-entry-content">
    <i>rāmakaṇṭha</i> [<a href="#entry-ka_n_tha" class="skt-link">kaṇṭha</a>] <a href="#entry-raamaka_n_tha" class="skt-link">m.</a> hist. np. de Rāmakaṇṭha <a href="#entry-Ubha_t_ta" class="skt-link">Bhaṭṭa</a>, théologien du <a href="#entry-Ukazmiira" class="skt-link">Kaśmīra</a> du 10⟨sup⟩e⟨/sup⟩ siècle (~950–~1000); il est l'auteur de la <a href="#entry-Uparamok_saniraasakaarikav_rtti" class="skt-link">Paramokṣanirāsakārikavṛtti</a> et d'un commentaire du <a href="#entry-Umatafgaparamezvaraagama" class="skt-link">Mataṅgaparameśvarāgama</a>.
  </div>
</div>

<div class="skt-entry" id="entry-raamakathaa">
